| ~~**W8-43**~~ | `:236` | **FIXED 2026-08-18 — checker-clean pure Chezzi could kill the process by host stack overflow, uncatchable by `recover:`, because a user `eq`/`str` hook re-entered the native structural walker with a FRESH depth-0 budget.** Neither guard could fire: call depth stayed far below its 10 000 cap while the *product* of hook-nesting depth × per-hook walk depth grew unbounded. Measured on the pre-fix release binary — a `Leaf.eq` that compares two freshly-built 100-link chains, nested 5 000 deep: `fatal runtime error: stack overflow, aborting`, **rc=134, core dumped**, and the `print("still alive")` after the `recover:` NEVER ran. CPython 3.14 on the same shape gives a catchable `RecursionError: Stack overflow (used 8148 kB) in comparison` — rc=0, process alive; Python is the owning ancestor for protocol semantics and its model is ONE recursion budget shared across nested re-entries. The defect **defeated the stated contract of the constant itself** (`src/vm/mod.rs`: this bound "turns that into a recoverable `RuntimeError`") and was **documented as deliberate in two source comments** — `values_equal_guarded`'s "starts a FRESH depth-0 walk … bounded there by the call-depth guard" and `stringify_into`'s "a `str` method re-stringifies at the *same* depth" — which is why no review ever questioned it. **No standing gate could reach it**: a SIGABRT has no assertion to fail, and while the CPython differential has classified signal-kills as findings since W7-33, `src/difftest/generate.rs` has no user-struct-with-protocol-method feature, so a hook-nested walk is structurally outside its corpus | **Fix: one shared budget.** New `Vm::walk_base` (per-`Vm`, beside `native_reentry` and for the identical reason — a fiber cannot park while a native re-entry is on the host stack, so no other fiber can observe it) holds the depth the enclosing walks already consumed; all **7** `MAX_STRUCTURAL_DEPTH` comparisons now test `walk_base + depth`, and the **4** `run_proto` dispatch sites that have a live structural `depth` in scope (the `eq` hook, and the `str` hook's struct/enum/newtype arms) go through a new panic-safe `Vm::guarded_walk`. The `catch_unwind` in that helper is load-bearing: `guarded` resumes an unwind from inside itself, so a plain post-call restore would leak `walk_base` onto a shell `Vm` that `run_one_fiber` keeps alive for the NEXT fiber. The cheaper alternative — charge `depth` to `call_depth` around each hook — was rejected because `GenCtx` carries its own `call_depth` and the generator resume swaps it away. Post-fix the repro prints `still alive` at **rc=0** with a recoverable `maximum structural depth (10000) exceeded`. Not charged: `hash`/`struct_compare` (O(1) native frames per `run_proto` — already `MAX_CALL_DEPTH`'s job) and `msort_indices_structs` (O(log n) frames per call level; ceiling recorded in a `ponytail:` comment beside the recursion). **CORRECTED 2026-08-18 (adversarial review, finding B1) — charging two of the seven guards was itself a silent wrong answer, and has been reverted.** Two guards do not fault, they DEGRADE: `cyclic_walk` (`src/vm/arith.rs:1415`) returns `true` (store the map/set key by reference) and `snapshot_value` (`:1477`) aliases the tail. The first cut charged `walk_base` to both and defended it as "the correct stack-safety direction"; it is not. **Charging a guard whose over-budget branch DEGRADES rather than FAULTS converts a stack-safety measure into a silent wrong value**, because an ORDINARY, shallow, acyclic key is pushed into the degrade branch merely because it was inserted from inside an `eq`/`str` hook running at depth — the key is then stored by reference, and the caller's later mutation reaches the stored key with no fault to show for it. Measured on the branch binary, one nesting level apart (`eq` hook inserts `k := K([1])` into `{k: 1}`, mutates `k`, reads the stored key back): depth 4998 → `true`, depth 4999 → **`false`**; pre-branch it is `true` at every depth. **The rule that survives: only a FAULTING guard may share the budget.** Both are back on the bare `depth` (which is also pre-branch semantics exactly, so the revert cannot introduce anything), and they stay charged IDENTICALLY to each other so `store_key_by_reference` and `snapshot_key` agree on which keys are over-deep. Their host-stack exposure at that seam is pre-existing and out of this row's scope. Pinned by `tests/chz/spec/eq_protocol_containers_test.chz::key_inserted_from_a_deep_eq_hook_is_still_snapshotted` at both depths |
| ~~**W8-44**~~ | dogfood | **CLOSED 2026-09-01 — a parameterized protocol bound now INFERS its type args from the witnessing type; it used to reject the call and blame a valid method.** As filed: `protocol Produces[R]: fn produce(self) -> R` / `struct IntProducer: fn produce(self) -> int` / `fn produce_as[R, T: Produces[R]](x: T) -> R` / `x := produce_as(IntProducer())` measured *type IntProducer does not satisfy Produces (method 'produce' has the wrong signature)* — factually false, `produce` satisfies `Produces[int]` exactly. `T` bound from the argument but nothing read `produce`'s return type back into `R`, so `enforce_bounds` compared against `Produces[R]` with `R` still a free `Ty::Param`. **Ancestor, run not reasoned:** the same program in Rust (`impl Produces<i32> for IntProducer`) compiles and prints `7` with no annotation; Rust emits `E0283: type annotations needed` only for a genuine two-impl ambiguity, which Chezzi cannot have — conformance is STRUCTURAL, so a type has one method per name and one witnessing signature. Post-fix the repro RUNS and prints `7` | **Fix: generalize a recovery the tree already had twice.** `recover_iter_elems` (`Iterator`/`Iterable`) and `recover_index_args` (`Index`/`IndexSet`/`Slice`) already read a parameterized bound's arg off the concrete type; the new `recover_protocol_args` does it for ANY user protocol by unifying each requirement signature against the witnessing method (`Self`-bound, receiver-params substituted — the same recipe `satisfies_methods` uses, so the two agree on what matches). Unification is one-directional, so only the requirement side carries `Ty::Param` and nothing the user wrote is rewritten; an arity or static-ness mismatch skips the method and recovers nothing. Wired into **both** `infer_generic_call` and `infer_generic_method` — one line each, the same seam as the two existing recoveries. The METHOD path gained the most: it has no `seed_from_hint`, so a result annotation could not pin `R` there either (`y: int = w.produce_as(…)` measured the false conformance error PLUS *cannot assign R to variable of type int*) and only turbofish worked. **THE GATE FOR THE FALLBACK DIAGNOSTIC IS A PROBE, NOT A STRUCTURAL TEST — the plausible-looking version is measurably too wide.** The first cut fired the new *cannot infer type parameter R* wherever a result-only param named by another param's bound was unbound. Measured, one program per shape: that is WRONG on three neighbours where `R` is also unbound but the method cannot conform for ANY instantiation — a wrong ARITY (`produce(self, extra: int)`), a wrong PARAM type (`conv(self, k: int)` vs `k: str`), and a MISSING method — because **adding the annotation the message recommends still fails there** (measured on all three), so the advice is a dead end printed above the true error. The shipped gate binds the candidates to `Ty::Unknown` (a wildcard through `compatible`/`method_matches`) and asks `enforce_bounds` whether the bound conforms GIVEN that hole — error-count delta before/after; only a satisfiable probe means the missing inference really is the cause. Post-fix each of the three reports its own true message ALONE. The diagnostic now owns exactly one residual: a protocol param named by NO requirement method. **One neighbour's message deliberately changed and the ancestor was re-run to settle it:** `x: int = produce_as(StrProducer())` moved from *wrong signature* to *cannot assign str to variable of type int*, because `StrProducer` genuinely satisfies `Produces[str]` — the error belongs to the annotation. Rust on the same program says `E0308: expected i32, found String`, an assignment error, not a trait error; the old message was itself part of this bug. Precedence unchanged (turbofish > args > recovery > annotation), and a recovered type that disagrees with an existing binding is DROPPED rather than reported, so a conflicting argument keeps its existing diagnostic. Pinned by four Rust tests + `tests/chz/spec/protocol_arg_inference_test.chz`; the three too-wide neighbours have their own `exactly one error` test, which is the shape the pre-fix corpus did not contain — `widening-untested-by-its-own-suite`. **Adversarial review (2 prosecutors) found 2 real defects in the first cut, both fixed before ship, and dismissed 3.** (a) **Name capture, HIGH** — a GENERIC witness method (`fn produce[U](self) -> List[U]` witnessing `Produces[R]`) recovered `R = List[U]`, binding the caller's param to a binder in no caller scope: measured, renaming that `U` to `W` flipped the program from compiling to `expected List[U], found List[W]`. Alpha-renaming must never change meaning. Fixed by skipping a witness with its own `type_params` — the same policy `satisfies_native` already applies (*native method '…' is generic and cannot witness a protocol requirement*). (b) **Duplicate diagnostics, MEDIUM** — the recovery calls `resolve_bound_arg`, which reports, and `enforce_bounds` re-resolves the same args immediately after, so `T: Produces[Bogus]` printed `unknown type 'Bogus'` TWICE on one call-site span; fixed with the paired `diag_mark`/`diag_rollback` (the speculative-pass idiom) rather than truncating a channel by hand. **Dismissed, each by measurement:** a claim that the probe emits dead-end advice for a builtin witness — the escapes DO work, the prosecutor annotated `int` where `List.pop()` gives `Option[int]`, so `v: int? = take(…)` and the matching turbofish both run; a claim that a protocol EXISTENTIAL argument fails to recover — `fn use(p: Produces[int]): f(p)` checks and prints `7`; and a claim that order-dependent blame on `two(IntP(), StrP())` reinstates the false message — the program is genuinely ill-typed for either order (`R` cannot be both `int` and `str`), so an error is correct and only WHICH operand is named is arbitrary. **Residuals recorded rather than closed** (all three: honest message, working escapes, no wrong answer): the recovery does not reach a **builtin** witness (`nominal_method_table` is struct/enum/newtype only — a builtin conforms through `satisfies_native`, whose table is built inline), nor a param supplied by an **embedded** protocol (`recover_bound_args_from` walks `pinfo.methods`, never `pinfo.embeds`), and the probe suppresses its message when an UNRELATED bound in the same call already failed (measured: the true error still surfaces — a missing diagnostic, never a wrong one). **TWO OF THOSE THREE ARE NOW CLOSED (2026-09-02).** The BUILTIN residual: `recover_bound_args_from` falls back to `self.structs[native_witness_key(ty)]` and rebuilds the table with the RECEIVER PREPENDED, exactly as `satisfies_native` does (`harvest_native_fn_sig` strips the leading bare `self` while a requirement keeps it as `params[0]`, so without the prepend the arity test is off by one and every native method is skipped) — measured, `take([1, 2, 3])` against `protocol Popper[R]: fn pop(self) -> R` went from *cannot infer type parameter R for 'take'* to running and printing `Some(3)`. It applies `satisfies_native`'s own two refusals — a generic native method, and one whose dispatch cannot reach the receiver — as SKIPS rather than errors, because this is a recovery and the conformance check reports for real immediately after. The EMBEDDED residual: the loop now walks `protocol_method_names` / `protocol_method_sig` instead of raw `pinfo.methods` — the existing transitive, cycle-guarded pair, which also RE-SPELLS an embedded signature into the owner's type-param vocabulary via `embed_arg_tys`. That re-spelling is load-bearing and is tested by naming the params DIFFERENTLY (`protocol Q[S]` embedding `Produces[S]` where `Produces` calls it `R`): a name-coincidence version would prove nothing. **The full W8-44 neighbour table was re-run and holds unchanged** — a missing method, a wrong arity, a wrong param type, and a non-conforming BUILTIN (`take(1)`, `take("abc")`) each still report their own true message ALONE, with no *cannot infer* above it. Pinned by `the_bound_arg_recovery_reaches_builtin_and_embedded_witnesses` plus two running `tests/chz` tests. **The third residual stays open by design:** the probe still suppresses its message when an unrelated bound in the same call already failed — a missing diagnostic, never a wrong one. **A FOURTH shape, found by adversarial review and also closed: a protocol EXISTENTIAL witness.** Measured, `p: Produces[int] = IntProducer()` then `produce_as(p)` gave *cannot infer type parameter R for 'produce_as'* while the annotated `v: int = produce_as(p)` ran and printed `8` — so the escapes worked but the annotation was one the ancestor does not ask for. (An earlier review DISMISSED a differently-shaped existential claim; that one passed the existential as an ARGUMENT to a fn that forwarded it, which does work. This one binds the bound's type param directly off the existential.) Fixed with the recipe `satisfies_methods`'s `Ty::Protocol` arm already uses: the method table comes from `protocol_method_names`/`protocol_method_sig` (so an existential of an EMBEDDING protocol witnesses too), the existential's own params bind to its carried args, and `Self` goes into the actual side's map as well. The full neighbour table was re-run and holds. **The PRE-EXISTING capture in the conformance check itself is now CLOSED TOO (2026-09-02).** When the caller's type param happened to share the witness method's own param NAME, `method_matches` compared the two `Ty::Param("U")`s as equal and the program conformed. **Repro, measured on `c0ab0a9e`** (the filed shape needed narrowing before it would reproduce — the caller's param must be reachable ONLY through the bound, or it binds from an argument and both spellings reject): `protocol Sink[U]: fn put(self, v: U) -> int` witnessed by `struct GS: fn put[U](self, v: U) -> int`, called from `fn use_sink[U, T: Sink[U]](x: T) -> int` as `use_sink(GS())` — **`ok: no type errors`**; renaming ONLY the struct method's own `U` to `W` gives *type GS does not satisfy Sink (method 'put' has the wrong signature)*. Alpha-renaming must never change meaning. **Fix: `method_matches` refuses any witness with its own `type_params`** — the same policy `satisfies_native` has always applied to builtins and `recover_bound_args_from` applies on the recovery side, with `satisfies_methods` gaining a matching arm so the message names the real reason (*method '…' is generic and cannot witness a protocol requirement*) instead of blaming an otherwise-correct signature. Freshening the binders instead gives the IDENTICAL verdict — a fresh name never equals the requirement's param — so refusing is the same semantics stated honestly, at a fraction of the machinery. **Ancestor run, not reasoned:** `trait Show { fn show(&self) -> String; }` with `impl Show for G { fn show<U>(&self) -> String }` is `E0049: method 'show' has 1 type parameter but its trait declaration has 0` — so Chezzi accepting it was drift, not a feature. **The narrowing's cost, measured:** a generic method witnessing a NON-parameterized protocol used to check clean and run (`fn show[U](self) -> str` under `protocol Show: fn show(self) -> str` printed `g`); it is now rejected, matching Rust. A sweep of every `.chz` in `examples/`, `tests/chz/`, `std/` and `benches/` pre-vs-post found **zero** newly-rejected files. One prior test was SUPERSEDED rather than worked around: `a_generic_witness_method_recovers_nothing` asserted the downstream *cannot infer type parameter R*, which the conformance refusal now pre-empts with the true cause; it asserts the new message and keeps the guard it actually existed for — that no diagnostic mentions a binder from a scope the caller cannot see. Pinned by `a_generic_method_cannot_witness_a_protocol`, which asserts BOTH spellings of the capture repro. **SECOND REVIEW ROUND (2 prosecutors + a defender, run on the fixed diff) upheld 2 more and dismissed 1.** (c) **The dedup fix was INCOMPLETE and its own regression test read green over the hole** — `probe_uninferable_dependent_result_params` also calls `resolve_bound_arg`, with no mark, so a bad bound arg was still printed twice whenever the signature HAD a result-only param; the test used `-> int`, which never turns the probe on, so it exercised one speculative pass and silently skipped the other. Fixed (second `diag_mark`/`diag_rollback`) and the test now asserts BOTH passes — the `widening-untested-by-its-own-suite` rule applies to the pass you widened, not just the function you edited. (d) **The fallback diagnostic is FREE-FN-ONLY, and the docs claimed otherwise** — `infer_generic_method` got `recover_protocol_args` but not the probe, so on a method call every recovery miss still prints the original false message. Measured: `v: int? = W().take([1,2,3])` against `Popper[R]` gives *List[int] does not satisfy Popper (method 'pop' has the wrong signature)* + *cannot assign R to variable of type Option[int]* where the free-fn twin RUNS and prints `Some(3)`; and `W().go(S())` with an unreachable param reports **`ok: no type errors`** with `R` left free where the free-fn twin names it. Docs corrected to state this rather than the reverse; NOT closed here because the message's *add a result annotation* half is false on that path until `seed_from_hint` is added to it, which is an inference change with its own neighbour table. **Dismissed:** that the probe's `Ty::Unknown` escaping into `subst_map` silently deletes downstream diagnostics — it does suppress them, but the call-site branch is EXHAUSTIVE (either `enforce_bounds` errored, or the inference message fires), so the call can never be check-clean and what is suppressed is cascade off an already-reported error; binding an un-inferable param to `Unknown` for exactly that reason is the established idiom `report_uninferable_closure_params` already uses. **METHOD-PATH GAP CLOSED (same day, follow-up commit) — the (d) residual above is FIXED, not documented.** `hint: Option<&Ty>` is now threaded into `infer_generic_method` (6 call sites in `src/checker/expr.rs`; `infer_method_call` already carried it from `expected`, so 5 needed only the extra argument and `resolve_native_handle_method` gained a forwarding param), `seed_from_hint` runs after the recoveries so precedence stays turbofish > arguments > recovery > annotation, and the probe-gated diagnostic is wired in behind it. Measured after: `v: int? = W().take([1,2,3])` RUNS and prints `Some(3)` (was *wrong signature* + *cannot assign R to variable of type Option[int]*), and an unreachable param now reports *cannot infer type parameter R for 'go'* (was `ok: no type errors` with `R` left free). The full neighbour table was re-derived on the METHOD path — M-N1..M-N6 — and every row matches the free-fn path exactly: the three could-never-conform shapes (missing method, wrong arity, wrong param type) each report their own true message ALONE, and `Pair[A,B]` runs. **One pre-existing assertion inverted and was corrected against the ancestor:** `return_only_method_type_param_stays_uninferable_rejected` demanded that `z: str = b.make()` on `fn make[U](self) -> U` be REJECTED with *cannot assign U to variable of type str*. That encoded the method path's MISSING hint as intended semantics — Rust compiles the identical program (`impl<T> Box<T> { fn make<U>(&self) -> U }`, `let z: String = b.make();`) and refuses only the UNANNOTATED `let z = b.make();` with `E0282: type annotations needed`. A false rejection on one spelling, never a wrong value, and the BOUND is enforced on both. Closing it means re-installing the hint per binary OPERAND the way `infer_if_else_chain` does per arm — an inference change touching every binary expression in a hinted position, with its own neighbour table. Measured after: `mkl[T]` and `mkl[Z]` against `G[T]` both check clean and both run, and the FIELD-default twin now reaches the same verdict for both spellings too (that verdict is a separate pre-existing limitation — a generic struct's provider-defaulted field is not optional in the positional ctor — identical for `T` on both binaries; what the test pins is that the two spellings AGREE, which they did not). **Neighbour table, each run on the pre-change binary:** a wrong-typed scalar default, a wrong-typed field default, a provider whose return cannot fill the slot, and a non-generic provider at a generic slot are ALL still rejected, unchanged; the generic-fn-VALUE default is unchanged in both spellings (the concrete-only hint gate above still governs it, so no new acceptance there and the arity trap that acceptance would have opened does not arise). **AN ACCEPT-WITHOUT-ENFORCE FOUND BY THE SAME TABLE, AND CLOSED BY HALF 1:** the decl-site copy never enforced the provider's own `where` bound — `fn mkl[Z: Show]() -> List[G[Z]]` defaulting a `List[G[T]]` was accepted with nothing showing `T: Show`, on BOTH spellings (so it was pre-existing, not something the renaming introduced), while the SAME call written out was correctly rejected with *type int does not satisfy Show*. Seeding creates the binding, so the existing `enforce_bounds` now fires with no new code — post-fix both spellings report *type T does not satisfy Show* at the declaration, which is the right place: the declaration is unusable for ANY `T` without the bound. **The escape works and was measured:** giving the ENCLOSING param the bound (`struct H[T: Show]`) compiles and runs, and an UNBOUNDED provider under a bounded enclosing param is fine — the bound is the provider's to demand, not to inherit. One prosecutor also correctly flagged that the working tree was being edited DURING its run, so its own HEAD measurements were taken against a moving binary — the repo's stale-binary trap, from the other side; its Call-default findings still held and were re-verified here on a settled build. `a_decl_site_default_is_still_type_checked` pins that the seeding did not become a rubber stamp — a wrong-typed parameter default, a wrong-typed field default, a generic provider whose return cannot fill the slot, and a concrete slot with a wrong element type all stay rejected. **The false rejection that fell out of the CONCRETE half is kept and is a strict improvement:** `fn run(x: int, f: fn(int) -> int = ident) -> int` on a generic `ident[T]` was *'ident' is generic and T is not determined here*; it now runs and prints `5`, because the default's declared type pins `T`. | ~~**W8-46**~~ | W8-45 review | **CLOSED 2026-09-01 — a constraining USE that dropped an empty collection's annotation requirement without PINNING its element type let a later use pin a different one, and seven routes built a heterogeneous collection check-clean at rc=0.** `drop_empty_site` clears the pending *cannot infer element type* requirement; three of its call sites also pinned, the rest did not, and the open `Unknown` slot was then free for a later use. Carved out of W8-45's own adversarial review, which measured its *root cause and fix, one site* claim FALSE. **Measured on `140c7041`, one program per route, every one `ok: no type errors` at rc=0:** (1) a VALUE ARM — `xs := []` / `n := if 1 < 2: addstr(xs) else: 0` (a `List[str]` parameter) / `xs.push(1)` / `ys: List[int] = xs` printed **`['a', 1]`** while the STATEMENT spelling was correctly rejected; (2) a GENERIC call whose parameter a SIBLING argument made concrete — `fn move_first[T](a: List[T], b: List[T])` called `move_first(["x"], xs)` then `xs.push(1)` printed **`['x', 1]`**; (3) an ANNOTATED LET sink — `ys: List[int] = xs` then `xs.push("a")` printed **`['a']`**; (4) a concrete RETURN sink — `fn give() -> List[str]: return zs` then `s.push("a")` / `zs.push(1)` printed **`['a', 1]`**; (5) a whole-binding REASSIGN — `b = [1, 2]` then `b.push("a")` printed **`[1, 2, 'a']`**; (6) a value ESCAPE nested in a literal — `c: List[List[int]] = [b]` then `b.push("a")` printed **`[['a']]`**; (7) an escape into a typed FIELD — `bx.items = b` (field `List[int]`) then `b.push("a")` printed **`['a']`**. Routes 5–7 were found by enumerating the seam rather than by re-reading the filed list, which named only three | **Fix: drop-and-pin is ONE operation.** `drop_empty_site` now takes a REQUIRED `shape: Option<&Ty>` and performs the `merge_unknown` + `repin` itself — the required parameter is the enforcement, exactly as `native::Kind`'s third tuple element is (omitting it is a compile error, which is the point), so a site added later cannot silently do half. Shapes supplied: the annotated `let`'s `declared`, the `return`'s `ret`, the reassign's `val_ty`, and — projected through the sink for a value escape — a list/set literal's element, a tuple's positional slot, a map's key/value (`drop_value_escape_sites` grew the same `Option<&Ty>`; the assign arm probes its TARGET's type behind a `diag_mark`/`diag_rollback`, gated on `empty_coll_sites` being non-empty so the ordinary path is untouched). `refine_receiver`/`refine_index_receiver` pass `None` and pin themselves below, because their drop must run BEFORE their own cascade guards; both say so at the call. The arg-side gate moved out of `check_args_range_decl` into `Checker::constrain_empty_arg` and is now called from `infer_generic_call`/`infer_generic_method` too, at the last moment `subst_map` can change — neither generic path routes through `check_args*`, which is the whole of route 2. **The arg gate had to TIGHTEN from `!contains_unknown_in_slot` to `ty_fully_concrete`:** a substituted generic slot can still be `List[T]` with `T` free, and `Ty::Param` is not `Ty::Unknown`, so the first cut pinned the binding to a rigid `List[T]` and reported *expected T, found int* on `fn ident[T](xs: List[T])` — a program that must pin nothing. Caught by the existing `ok()` neighbour, which is why that neighbour was written. **Route 1's fix is a DELETION, and the plausible repair was measured wrong.** Snapshotting `empty_coll_sites` alongside the binding types does NOT fix the value arm — it just forgets the constraining use entirely and lets the later push win (measured: still `['a', 1]`). The barrier itself was the defect: `snapshot_refinable`/`restore_refinable` are **deleted**, and a value-arm pin now persists exactly like statement position, which is what `docs/reference/syntax.md`'s scope-wide rule already promised everywhere else. A value arm is a single expression, so the only refinement reachable inside one is `constrain_empty_arg` — `push`/`add` and `m[k]=v` are statements and cannot appear there — which is why the barrier had so little left to protect. **Neighbour table for that narrowing, run on the pre-fix binary** (both newly-rejected rows were `ok: no type errors` before): two CONFLICTING concrete uses across sibling value arms reject, in the `if` spelling, the `match` spelling and for a `Map` binding — the same rule two conflicting pushes already follow, and the one Rust applies (a `Vec` cannot be both `Vec<String>` and `Vec<i32>`); AGREEING arms, an arm-only use, and an arm followed by a CONSISTENT later use all still check clean and run. **CEILING, measured and deliberately not closed — the un-annotated ALIAS.** `b := []` / `c := b` / `c.push(1)` / `b.push("a")` prints `[1, 'a']`, unchanged by all of the above: `c := b` has no concrete sink to pin from, so the requirement legitimately MOVES to `c` (the documented escape rule) and the two names are then pinned independently while sharing one runtime list. Closing it needs alias-identity tracking, which would newly REJECT a rebound alias (`c := b` then `c = [1, 2]` then `b.push("a")`) — a false rejection is worse than this missing one, and neither ancestor settles it: Rust forbids the shape outright (`let c = b` MOVES), Python has no static element type at all. Recorded in `docs/reference/syntax.md` as the one remaining sliver, replacing the value-arm sliver this row closed. Pinned by `every_constraining_use_pins_the_element_type` (all seven routes, the alias ceiling and its annotation escape) and `a_value_arm_pin_persists_like_statement_position` (the narrowing's full neighbour table). **ADVERSARIAL REVIEW (2 prosecutors) on the shipped commit found 3 real defects, all fixed, and re-scoped a fourth.** (a) **FALSE REJECTION, high, FIXED.** The pin's BINDING gate was the broad `contains_unknown_in_slot`, which also matches `Option[Unknown]` (`o := None`) and a nullary generic enum variant (`e := Box.Empty`) — values that record no site, ask for no annotation, and must stay permissive. Measured: `e := Box.Empty` / `a: Box[int] = e` / `b: Box[str] = e` went from running to *cannot assign Box[int] to variable of type Box[str]*, and the `None` twin the same. Fixed by cutting on `is_unrefined_empty_coll` — **the identical mistake W8-45(a) made in the opposite direction**, and the identical repair: the gate must be the shape the receiving machinery handles, never a superset that looks related. The fix also closes the same false rejection on the ARGUMENT path, where the wide gate was PRE-EXISTING (measured rejected on both binaries before). (b) **ORDER DEPENDENCE, critical, FIXED.** The assignment arm's target-type probe was gated on `empty_coll_sites.is_empty()` — written as a perf shortcut, but it decides whether the assignment PINS, so acceptance depended on an unrelated binding elsewhere in the file. Measured, two programs differing only in where an unrelated `z.push(1)` sits: before the assignment rc=0, after it *cannot assign Option[str] to variable of type Option[int]* rc=1. Now gated on `escapes_unrefined_empty(value)` — a property of THAT statement alone. (c) **A FALSE JUSTIFYING CLAIM, FIXED.** The barrier-removal note said `push`/`add` "are statements and cannot appear" in a value arm; `y := if c: xs.push(1) else: xs.push("a")` parses and does reach `refine_receiver`. It returns nil, so such a program is rejected either way — the removal changes which diagnostic it gets, not whether it is accepted — but the note was the sole stated justification and was simply wrong. (d) **RE-SCOPED, not fixed, by measurement.** The pin does not re-run `refine_receiver`'s `Hashable` ban, so `s := Set()` / `c: Set[float] = s` / `s.add(1.5)` loses one of its three errors. Run across every shape that can reach it (annotated sink, typed parameter, typed return), an illegal `Set[float]` cannot be SPELLED without erroring at the spelling site — the fact is always still reported (twice, at the annotation), so what is lost is a THIRD copy, never the only one. Restoring it would print the same fact three times; recorded instead, with a test pinning that the program stays rejected. **The alias ceiling was also understated and is now documented honestly:** it is not a benign mixed print — the two static types disagree about a shared value, so `fn addstr(xs: List[str]) -> str: return xs[0].upper()` called on the unpinned half is `ok: no type errors` and then *type int has no method 'upper'* at run time. `docs/reference/syntax.md` carries that repro, the annotation escape that closes it, and the reason the checker fix is refused. A prosecutor also swept every `.chz` in `examples/`, `tests/`, `std/` and `benches/` pre-vs-post: **zero diffs**, so the narrowing costs the corpus nothing TICKET-032 (A1) closes the one route `drop_empty_site` cannot reach: an un-annotated ALIAS (`c := b`) moved the pending requirement to `c` alone, so the two names pinned independently while sharing one runtime list — `c.push(1)` then a typed `addstr(b)` (param `List[str]`) was `ok: no type errors`, then faulted at run time with *type int has no method 'upper'*. A new `(scope, name)`-keyed `empty_coll_aliases` pin group (drained at `pop_scope`, untainted at `declare`, unlinked in `check_assign`'s funnel) propagates a pin across the pair; a whole-binding reassignment or re-declaration of either name still breaks it, so a rebound alias is never falsely rejected. |
| ~~**W8-47**~~ | W8-44 review | **CLOSED 2026-09-02 — a non-inline default whose slot type mentioned a type parameter made that parameter/field non-omittable even when a turbofish had already pinned the binder, and the filed root cause was measured FALSE.** As filed: `struct Holder[T]: n: int; items: List[G[T]] = mkl()` (on `fn mkl[Z]() -> List[G[Z]]`) then `Holder(1)` reports *Holder() expects 2 argument(s), got 1*. Two SEPARATE mechanisms, not one: a **free fn** (`fn conv[T](a: int, b: List[T] = mkl())`) is rejected by an exact arity test in `infer_generic_call` (`args.len() != sig.params.len()`), while `FnSig::min_params`/`ast::min_callable_params` already computes the shrunk count and every non-generic path already uses it. A **struct ctor** has a different cause: `desugar::dflt_for` returns `Dflt::CalleeFilled` for the field, `normalize_call` omits the argument (correct for a fn, which has a callee prologue to fill it from), but `Op::NewStruct` takes its field count from the CALL SITE, so the ctor call is genuinely short by one argument and the checker's own arity check (correctly) refuses it. The filed root cause — `min_callable_params` counting a defaulted trailing param as required, the message coming from `check_args_range_decl` — was measured FALSE on both counts | **Fix, two independent commits.** **Free fn:** `infer_generic_call`'s gate is now `!(sig.min_params..=sig.params.len()).contains(&args.len())`; a witness-taking callee (`fn conv[T: Default](a: int, b: List[T] = List())`, body calling `T.default()`) keeps its full arity because `min_callable_params` returns `params.len()` whenever the sig carries witness params — pinned by `w8_47_a_witness_taking_callee_keeps_its_full_arity` beside the pre-existing `witness_forwarding_still_charges_every_unpinned_shape_rejected`. Before/after: `conv[int](1)` and `conv(1)` both go from *conv() expects 2 argument(s), got 1* to `ok: no type errors` (witness-free); the witness-taking shape stays rejected. **Struct ctor: the arity check is NOT lifted** — a ctor has no callee, so `desugar` no longer leaves one short when the turbofish is complete instead. New `Dflt::GenericProvider`: a field default whose type mentions the struct's own UNBOUNDED type parameters gets a provider generic in those parameters, and the ctor's own turbofish is FORWARDED to it (`normalize_call` declines the splice when the counts don't match, keeping today's short call). Before/after: `Holder[int](1)` goes from *Holder() expects 2 argument(s), got 1* to `ok`, prints `1` then `0`. `Holder(1)` (no turbofish to forward) now reports the W8-45-style *cannot infer type parameter T for 'Holder'; the default for field 'items' can only be filled with explicit type arguments, e.g. Holder[int](...)* — matching Rust's `error[E0282]: type annotations needed for Holder<_>` on the same shape — needed a new `StructInfo.defaulted_fields` so the checker can tell an omitted DEFAULTED field from a missing required one. **Residual, measured and accepted:** a BOUNDED owner type parameter (`struct Holder[T: Show]`) keeps the plain arity refusal even with a turbofish written — a generic provider carrying the bound would raise a witness question `dflt_for` cannot answer before types exist, and "add explicit type arguments" would be FALSE advice there since the turbofish is already present. **Widening, measured and covered:** giving a generic field a provider means the provider BODY is now judged for a `?` in the default (DEC-025), which the field decl-site copy never did — `items: List[G[T]] = mkl()?` on a `!str`-returning `mkl` went from `ok: no type errors` to *a default expression cannot propagate with `?`*. **Test-helper gotcha:** `ok()`/`check_src` skips `desugar` entirely, so the committed ctor test (`w8_47_struct_ctor_default_arity_is_binder_blind`) moved to `ok_desugared` — under `check_src` every defaulted field is unfilled regardless of the fix |

| ~~**W9-1**~~ | `W9 log` | **CLOSED 2026-09-04 (TICKET-051) — a crossed closure no longer keeps a private copy of the module globals.** As filed: the airlock installed a frozen `gsnap` onto a closure that crossed via `Channel.send`/`spawn`, and `Op::GetGlobalSlot` preferred it over the live slot forever after, so inside ONE task a module global denoted TWO objects — three with the task's own copy. Four measured shapes, all `check` rc=0, all identical at `CHEZZI_THREADS=1`/`2`/default: a `.push` through the closure never reached the task's own global (`2 / 3 / 1`, CPython `2 / 3 / 3`); a closure allocated INSIDE the task after its own write, crossing nothing, still read the pre-write value (`1 / 100`, Go and CPython `100 / 100`) because `Op::MakeClosure` cloned the parent's `gsnap`; two closures from one factory disagreed in one task at one instant; and a module-global generator silently re-yielded `1` (`1 / 2 / 1`, CPython `1 / 2 / 3`). **Fix:** `gsnap` is DELETED; the airlock installs the sender's free globals into the RECEIVING task's own module view, filtered `assigned \|\| carried`. `assigned`-only was tried and killed by plan-validation on a true counterexample. Supersedes one paragraph each of DEC-016 and DEC-041 — DEC-041's stated ground ("neither reference language poses that question ... there is no oracle for it") was measured false: Go 1.26.5 and CPython 3.14 both answer with the sender's live value. | ✅ CLOSED — verified post-merge on the release binary at three worker counts |
| ~~**W9-2**~~ | `W9 log` | **CLOSED 2026-09-04 (TICKET-052) — a blocking `Executor` job no longer pins its pool thread.** As filed: an eager job did not yield its slot on the bounded process-wide pool while blocked on a `Channel`/`Shared`/`sleep_ms`, so K blocking jobs plus one unblocker hung FOREVER whenever K ≥ worker count — silently, with no fault, no exit code, and no `--timeout` escape (`--timeout` is `chezzi test`-only). Reproduced at the DEFAULT worker count on ordinary code: 13 consumers + 1 feeder on a 12-core box, rc=124. Nested `Executor`s needed one thread per level. Measured vehicle split: 3×`sleep_ms(500)` as jobs = 1511/1021/519 ms at T=1/2/4 (serialised); the same as `spawn` tasks = 516/512/507 ms (parked correctly). `GOMAXPROCS=1` Go and `max_workers=1` CPython complete every shape. **Fix:** a pool thread about to block spawns a replacement and RETIRES when its job ends, bounded at one replacement per job; an OS-refused replacement blocks in place, i.e. degrades to the pre-fix behaviour rather than faulting. | ✅ CLOSED — verified post-merge; both hang repros now complete |
| ~~**W9-3**~~ | `W9 log` | **CLOSED 2026-09-04 (TICKET-053) — an unmet `eq` `where`-bound can no longer be laundered through a protocol slot.** As filed: `Box[T]` declaring `fn eq(self, o: Box[T]) -> bool where T: Comparable` was correctly refused on a direct `Box[Tag] == Box[Tag]`, but assigning both to a `Tagged`-typed slot first and comparing THOSE was accepted and ANSWERED — `true`, `check` rc=0, where structural `==` on the same values is `false`. Also fired through a `[T: Eq]` bound and an `Option[Tagged]` payload. Rust rejects at compile time (`E0369`); Go panics (`comparing uncomparable type`); Chezzi was the only one of the three that returned a value. **Fix:** the gate lives in `Checker::assignable`'s protocol arm — the ERASURE is the last point at which the concrete witness is visible; at the comparison it is gone by design, and gating there would refuse every protocol-typed comparison and break `Result[T]`, whose error slot is `Ty::Protocol("Error")`. Deliberately the where-bound half only, not the strict walk, so a witness with no `eq` still compares structurally. **This also falsified two live claims:** `docs/contributing/gaps.md` W7-52's "cleanly faulting when it can't" and `tests/chz/spec/eq_protocol_existential_test.chz`'s "never a silent wrong answer" header — that test had been written to avoid the case that falsifies it. | ✅ CLOSED — verified post-merge, incl. the `Box[int]` over-fire control |
| ~~**W9-4**~~ | `W9 log` | **CLOSED 2026-09-05 (TICKET-054) — three sinks now use an expected type that was already statically known.** All false REJECTIONS of programs the ancestors accept: `x: Sh = if true: Sq(2) else: Tr(9)` was `branches have incompatible types` though `xs: List[Sh] = [Sq(2), Tr(9)]` was fine (Rust and Go both print `4`); `g := fn() -> float: 3` was refused though `fn b() -> float: 3` and the same method widen, and though success-coercion at the identical sink DOES fire on closures; and `p.m(1)` through a protocol-typed receiver or a bound type parameter was refused though `s.m(1)` on the concrete receiver widens. **Fix:** consult the hint on a `compatible` mismatch only, and only through `assignable`, with the hint required `ty_fully_concrete`. **Deliberately NOT widened:** `x: float = if true: 1 else: 2` stays rejected — nothing in the backend coerces an if-expression branch, so accepting it would re-open DEC-034's un-coerced-float hole. That is the checker⊋compiler rule holding: do not accept what the compiler cannot lower. | ✅ CLOSED — verified post-merge (`4` / `3.0` / `1.0`) |
| ~~**W9-5**~~ | `W9 log` | **CLOSED 2026-09-05 (TICKET-055) — `newtype` now follows DEC-029 like `struct` and `enum`.** **The ticket as filed was WRONG and is the row to read before re-filing anything in this area.** It claimed the duplicate-declaration guard "misses" `struct S` + `fn S`. That pair is DEC-029, an ACTIVE decision of 2026-08-30 shipped as `std.path`'s public API (`std/path.chz:36`, `fn Path(p: PathLike) -> Path`), and the filed fix would have deleted a working feature. Measured, three hosts: `struct S` + `fn S` → fn wins (`6`); `enum E` + `fn E` → fn wins (`21`); `newtype N` + `fn N` → the CTOR wins (`N(2)`). Two of three already implemented DEC-029; newtype was the lone outlier. CPython settles the direction — `class S` then `def S` prints `6`, class orphaned, no error — so rejecting the pair would have diverged from the owning ancestor to enforce an unmeasured assertion. **Fix:** four gates (checker bare/qualified, compiler bare/qualified) made to agree for newtype, and the TICKET-029 review's opposite pin on the qualified spelling flipped, with the reason recorded rather than silently undone. **Residual, deliberately out of scope:** with `fn S(n: int) -> int` the struct becomes unconstructible everywhere, which DEC-029 never contemplated; if ever surfaced it is a WARNING, never an error, and needs its own ticket with the warn/silent split derived by RUNNING every position. | ✅ CLOSED — verified post-merge, incl. controls that `struct S`+`fn S` and `std/path.chz` are untouched |
| ~~**W9-6**~~ | `W9 log` | **CLOSED 2026-09-05 (TICKET-056) — a generic fn can now forward its own type parameter by explicit turbofish.** As filed: `fn outer[T: Mk](n: int) -> int: return inner[T](n)` was rejected with *"no hidden type witness for 'T' is reachable at this call site. One exists only where 'T' is declared by a FUNCTION or a MEMBER"* — a message stating the exact condition that was satisfied. The INFERRED spelling of the same forward already worked (`witness_static_call_forwarding_ok`), and the three deliberately-refused shapes (enclosing TYPE param, unbounded param, fn-value position) are none of them. No workaround existed when the callee's `T` appears only as a static-method receiver. Rust prints `40 / 40`. **Fix:** an explicit turbofish charges the enclosing declaration's own witness parameter even when that parameter is absent from the enclosing signature; the `ty_param_in_sig` fence stays for the inferred channel. The widening can only affect programs that already failed to compile, so no green program loses a position. The diagnostic was also split by who declared the parameter, so it no longer describes a superset of what it grants. | ✅ CLOSED — verified post-merge (`40 / 40`) |
| ~~**W9-7**~~ | `W9 log` | **CLOSED 2026-09-05 (TICKET-057) — `json.parse` names where the TOKEN BEGAN, not where the scan stopped.** As filed: `json.parse('"hello')` reported `char 6` where CPython reports `char 0`; `nll` reported `char 15` where CPython reports `char 14`. A 274-document mutation sweep found 44 mismatches, partitioned exactly by class: every multi-character token error was wrong (21/21 unterminated string, 8/8 `true`, 8/8 `null`, 3/3 fraction, 3/3 exponent) and every single-character error was right (0/217). This contradicted both `docs/reference/stdlib.md`'s "the same position CPython names" and the CLOSED row W8-36, and it contradicted the repo's own lexer rule M24-7. **Fix:** the four sites capture a NAMED start position. A 1086-document sweep took the mismatch count from 242/918 to 26/918; the 26 are three deliberate, measured divergences (trailing comma needs a new diagnostic class and emitting the existing message at the comma would name `]` at a position holding `,`; an invalid escape names the escape char per rustc and M24-7; a leading zero names the numeral start). Trap recorded: on `[1.5e+]` the exponent branch consumes the sign, so `self.pos - 1` names the `+`, not the `e`. | ✅ CLOSED — verified post-merge against CPython on both cases |
| ~~**W9-8**~~ | `W9 log` | **CLOSED 2026-09-05 (TICKET-058) — a leading UTF-8 BOM is stripped, as CPython and Go both do.** As filed: a file whose bytes are `EF BB BF` + `print(1)` gave `lex error: unexpected character '\u{feff}'` at 1:1, naming an invisible character with no mention of a BOM — and an imported module carrying one broke the whole build. Windows editors and VS Code write one routinely. **The fix is wider than the lexer, and that is the finding inside the finding:** FIVE consumers index raw source by a lexer `(line, col)`, so stripping only at the lexer would have shifted `end_col`, the caret, hover hits and semantic-token lengths by one on line 1 — measured RED in a scratch copy before the plan shipped (`left: (0, 6, 7)` against `right: (0, 6, 10)`; the number token missing from the semantic-token vector). Strip is `strip_prefix`, never `trim_start_matches`: CPython raises `SyntaxError: invalid non-printable character U+FEFF` on a SECOND leading BOM and on a mid-file one, both measured. A BOM-only file is an empty program at rc 0, matching CPython. | ✅ CLOSED — verified post-merge (`1`, rc=0) |
| ~~**W9-9**~~ | `W9 log` | **CLOSED 2026-09-05 (TICKET-059) — the ratchet now scans Rust tests too, and a sleep used to ORDER two events is banned alongside a clock read.** As filed: `tests/no_wall_clock_ratio_gates.rs` scans `Path::new("tests/chz")`, so TICKET-049's ratio ban and TICKET-050's allowlist both stop at the Chezzi suite and never look at `tests/*.rs` or `#[cfg(test)]` modules under `src/`. Measured at load average 28.9: fixed `0.776-0.946` cores against `1.267-1.771` reverted. The nested fixture is 16 spawns because at 8 one reverted run in eleven read `0.755`, inside the fixed band. | ~~**W7-58**~~ | `:9538` | **FIXED 2026-08-12 — a nursery owner is now a counted party AND a judge, so a run stuck across the nursery/executor boundary faults instead of hanging.** Measured: the repro (a job blocked forever on one channel, a `spawn`ed task blocked forever on another) went **rc=124 at 20 s** → **rc=1 at 7 ms**, `deadlock … the nursery cannot progress`. Root cause was arithmetic, not a missing veto: `quiesced` (`quiesce.rs:218`) computes `live = 1 + outstanding_jobs`, and the `1 +` is the allowance for `main` — but a `main` inside a nursery join never registers (`owns_os_thread()` requires `mn.is_none() && mn_enlist_sched.is_none() && scheduler_stack.is_empty()`, false by construction), so `parties.len() = 1 < live = 2` vetoed forever. Proof it was exactly that: the identical program with the `parallel:`/`spawn` removed, `main` doing the `recv` directly, **faulted correctly at 12 ms** | **Fix, three parts.** **R1** — `is_deadlocked` splits: the `W7-56` job veto stays first and byte-identical, then delegates to a new `is_deadlocked_ignoring_jobs`. **R2** — a new `PartyWait::Nursery(Arc<MnSched>)`, satisfiable ⟺ `!sched.is_deadlocked_ignoring_jobs(&c)`, evaluated **live** at every verdict (a snapshot would be `quiesce.rs`'s build-bug #1, which faulted 6/10). Registered by `Vm::nursery_party_guard`, RAII-scoped to the join at four owner-loop sites; the gate is `mn.is_none() && scheduler_stack.is_empty()`, deliberately NOT `is_counted_party` (whose `mn_enlist_sched.is_none()` clause wrongly excludes an enlisted builder). **A worker shell must never register** — it is not in `live`, and registering it would let `parties.len()` exceed `live`, the one direction that faults a live program. **R3** — the owner also JUDGES: a nursery owner never reaches `block_halt_check`, so `take_runnable` escalates to `quiesced()` when the sched is stuck-modulo-jobs. **The `drop(c)` before `quiesced()` is load-bearing** — `quiesced` takes `parties` then, through `Nursery::satisfiable`, that same core lock; the total order is `sched_registry → parties → SchedCore → exec_registry → eager → ChannelCore::q`. **The row's own claim that a nursery `PartyWait` "would need `satisfiable() == !is_deadlocked()`, which is circular" was WRONG** — it needs `!is_deadlocked_ignoring_jobs()`, which reads no party state and no `outstanding` and is therefore a pure function of `SchedCore`. The circularity was in the coarse predicate, not in the idea. **Two high-severity defects caught by adversarial review** (three prosecutors, two converging independently), both reproduced before acceptance: (1) **the judge was EDGE-triggered** — after judging it parked in an untimed `cv.wait`, and nothing notifies a sched when a *party registers*, so a verdict that became true later was never asked (**5/5 deterministic hangs** on two repros; the tell was that adding a third job whose `finish` pokes the sched made the same program fault). Fixed by bounding the idle wait to `DEMOTE_POLL_BACKOFF` **only when `judged` is set** — the healthy path keeps its untimed wait and gains no poll. (2) the new join-cycle test was a **coin flip** (7/40 completing normally) because the barrier gated the `recv`s but not the `shutdown` that decides cycle-vs-early-return; rebuilt as a self-join ring so the fault always reaches the one slot vec `main` reduces — 40/40. **Also fixed inside this change, not filed:** `join_eager_jobs` registered a party and then waited on a plain untimed condvar, never asking the verdict it had just made answerable — three executors whose jobs each `shutdown()` the next hung forever. It now polls at `DEMOTE_POLL_BACKOFF` and asks, but **only when every registered party is a `Join`** (`quiesced_only_joins`); without that restriction it races a channel-blocked party and `two_executors_deadlocking_each_other_fault` flips between two messages. **False-fault evidence** (the bar `W7-12` set — three predicates shipped green and faulted healthy programs at 2–7/30): the cap-1 producer/consumer pipeline across a job and a nursery task is **30/30 at default width and 30/30 at `CHEZZI_THREADS=2`, both directions**; the `W7-56` healthy repro 30/30; all 48 concurrency `examples/` identical rc and wall time. **A socket-blocked job still vetoes, and that is correct, not a gap** — `sched.rs:1364-1380` records that Go treats an open socket as a runnable party, so `all goroutines are asleep` does not fire there either |
| ~~**W7-56**~~ | `:9538` | **FIXED 2026-08-12 — an eager `Executor` job can now feed a nursery task, instead of the nursery declaring the live program deadlocked.** Measured, M:N: the repro went **`deadlock`, rc=1 at 7 ms (the feeder never ran)** → **`job sending` / `child got 7` / `after nursery`, rc=0 at 58 ms**, matching Go. **It was TWO defects sharing one repro, and the second is the load-bearing one.** (1) `MnSched::is_deadlocked` (`src/vm/mod.rs:3045`) has five gates and **no `ExecRegistry` term** — an outstanding job is invisible to it. (2) **An eager job's `send` could not reach a fiber parked on an `MnSched` at all.** With the predicate held vetoed by an unrelated `inflight` sibling, the program printed `child waiting` / `job sending` (50 ms — the send DID happen) / `sleeper done` (2000 ms) and *then* faulted `deadlock` at 2008 ms **with the value sitting in the queue**: `spawn_worker` never sets `mn` (the only `mn` assignment is `spawn_shell`, `sched.rs:877`), so a job's `Vm` takes `channel_send_wire`'s no-sched branch, and `wake_on_send` returned immediately on an empty `scheduler_stack` while the waiter sat in `SchedCore::parked`, drainable only by `MnSched::wake_bucket`. **So a predicate-only veto would have converted the wrong answer into a PERMANENT SILENT HANG** (idle workers `cv.wait` untimed, `mod.rs:2445`) — verified by mutation, 3/3 runs rc=124 | **Fix, three parts that must ship together.** **R1** — `MnSched` gains `exec_registry`, assigned AFTER construction (the existing `parent_wake` pattern) so all seven unit fixtures stay untouched, and `is_deadlocked` vetoes on `QuiesceState::outstanding_jobs(&self.exec_registry) > 0` — the SAME veto `quiesce::quiesced` already applies process-wide (`parties.len() < live`, `quiesce.rs:218-229`) for the same reason: a live job is an uncounted sender. Reused, not re-derived; **no new counter** (`quiesce.rs:25-28` says why). **R2** — a run-wide `Vm::sched_registry: Arc<Mutex<Vec<Weak<MnSched>>>>` walked in **`Vm::wake_on_send` and nowhere else**, because that fn is the shared tail of all FIVE no-sched wake sites (`close`, `trip`, `channel_send_wire`, the bounded enqueue, the bounded slot-free wake) — patching `channel_send_wire` alone would have left four broken. `Weak`, so teardown needs no deregistration. **R3** — `dispatch_eager_job`'s completion pokes every live sched (lock briefly, drop, `notify_all`; the brief lock is what supplies the happens-before against a worker that read `outstanding == 1` and is about to `cv.wait`), so a job that ends WITHOUT sending lets the genuine verdict fire instead of hanging — measured 58 ms, still a fault. **Lock order** verified as one total order `sched_registry → A → exec_registry → eager → q`; nothing holds `exec_registry`/`eager` and then takes a sched core lock. **Accepted cost, measured, not papered over:** a genuine nursery deadlock whose outstanding job is ALSO blocked forever now **hangs** where it used to fault at 7 ms (`parked-is-not-stuck`'s own ordering: correct > silent > **wrong**, and the old fault was right by accident from the same unsound reasoning). Neither the parked fibers nor a `main` inside a nursery join is a counted party, so no second detector fires — filed as **`W7-58`**. Also measured: **+7% on channel send/recv** (200k pairs, 147 ms → 158 ms) from one uncontended lock per no-sched `send`; no bench covers that path. **`--serial` is unchanged and unfixable here** — it never dispatches eagerly (`netio.rs:3459`; D3 = queue-at-submit, drain-at-`shutdown`), so the feeder genuinely cannot run before the join and the program is a REAL deadlock there; every new test is M:N-only for that reason, and out of `parity_tests.rs`/`tests/chz/` which are gated serial==M:N |
| ~~**W7-57**~~ | `:9538` | **FIXED 2026-08-12 — a run-wide `os.exit` now reaches every party, not just the polling ones.** **The row as filed UNDERSTATED this: it claimed "none is a correctness hazard — the exit code is right in every case, only its latency is wrong." Both halves were false.** Measured on the pre-fix binary vs Go 1.26.5: a **spinning** nursery task → **rc=124, HANGS FOREVER** (not slow — never exits); a **`recv`-parked** task and a **`sleep_ms(3000)`** task → rc=3 but at **3012 ms** *and each printed a completion line Go never prints* (`keepalive done`, `slow child finished`). Go is rc=3 at ~53 ms in all three, printing neither. Two further shapes the row never named also hung forever: a spinning **top-level `main`** and a spinning **eager job** | **The fix needed almost no new machinery — the checkpoints already existed.** They are all `cancel_requested()` sites; what was missing is that `request_exit` never ran the teardown an *intra-nursery* `os.exit` has run since B3.4 (`sched.rs:1642`). Now `request_exit` publishes the code, then walks `W7-56`'s `sched_registry` calling a new `MnSched::cancel_all` (trip every scope's cancel **under the core lock** — the release edge `trip_scope_cancel` documents; a bare store outside it lets a worker read a stale `false` and reap the scope as `Deadlocked`, dropping its `defer`s), then `cancel_drain` + `poller::drain_sched` + `notify_all`. That single push closes the parked, sleeping, netpoller-parked and in-callback shapes with **zero new polling loops**; `time.sleep_ms` needed no new chunking because W7-16's `arm_timer_sleep` already re-arms every `DEMOTE_POLL_BACKOFF` reading exactly the flags `cancel_all` trips. Only the CPU-bound shapes needed a new rung: a 1/1024-sampled `AtomicBool` check on the loop back-edge (`jump_checked`) and in `Vm::guarded` (the per-element native-HOF re-entry), for the two parties no cancel reaches — top-level `main` (no scope) and an eager job (whose only flag is its executor's `shutdown_now` token, which an `os.exit` must not trip). **Measured after: all eight shapes rc=3 at 61-68 ms**, no leaked completion lines, agreeing with Go. `--serial` is byte-identical (it never dispatches an eager job at `submit`, so the exiting party does not exist while the nursery runs — every new test is M:N-only for that reason). **Three defects caught by adversarial review** (three prosecutors, two converging), all reproduced before acceptance: (1) **a cancelled sibling's `defer` was destroyed, sometimes HALF-RUN** — measured `SIB DEFER RAN` 0/10 post-fix vs **6/6 pre-fix**, and a body that printed `cleanup ENTER` then died mid-loop; it also broke serial↔M:N agreement (serial still ran it). A half-executed cleanup is worse than either extreme, so the fix suppresses the rung while `deferring > 0` **at the `run_exit_err` funnel** (which also covered four pre-existing `W7-47` blocking rungs nobody had charged) and routes the CPU rungs through a new `Vm::exit_halt` that takes the **cancel** path for a fiber holding any scope flag — now `whole 30/30, partial 0/30`. (2) **`exit_pending` poisoned every later SUITE test and hook** — `invoke_test` cleared it but `invoke_suite_method`/`build_suite_instance` did not, so `ERROR B::b_loops … exit` where pre-fix it PASSED; this is `W7-47`'s defect #1 one layer down, escalated by the new flag from "later *blocking* tests" to anything with a loop or a HOF. Fixed at the root in a shared `Vm::reset_for_invoke`. (3) a memory-ordering claim that did not hold — an `Acquire` load orders only *subsequent* reads and `jump_checked` reads cancel *before* exit, so the publication order never guaranteed the rung order; the claim is deleted, not softened. **Also closed, the asymmetry this change itself created:** `Vm::guarded` gained an exit rung but had no deadline rung, so `chezzi test --timeout=500` reported **`PASS` on a native-HOF test that ran 1985 ms** — a cap silently not applying, which teaches distrust of every green run. `guarded` now runs `deadline → cancel → exit`, matching `jump_checked`. **Perf:** the rung had to go in a `#[cold] #[inline(never)]` helper — inlined it cost **+4.5% on `loop.chz`, a bench that never calls `guarded` at all** (pure code-layout perturbation, isolated by deleting exactly those lines). Final independent A/B (hyperfine, 20 runs): `loop` +1.0%, `list` +1.0%, and **`fib` — the zero-loop control the rung cannot touch — also +1.2%**, so the movement is layout noise, not the change. **A measured limit of the constraints, now DOCUMENTED and made honest rather than filed:** a fiber inside an offloaded **blocking-pool** native (`process.cmd("sleep 5")`) is in a libc call whose only checkpoint is at entry. Measured identical for all three halts — sibling fault/cancel 5013 ms, `os.exit` 5015 ms, `--timeout=500` 5008 ms — so it is **not exit-specific**, and it is the deliberate price of offloading these calls so they never pin a core worker. Reaching it needs a signal, an fd close, or `process::exit`, and `stream.rs:17-20` refuses the last for documented reasons. Two things were done about it instead of a follow-up row (2026-08-12): (1) **`docs/reference/stdlib.md` now documents it** — a shared *"Blocking calls cannot be interrupted"* section covering the `std.io` file seams, every `std.fs` syscall, every `std.process` runner and every `std.request` call, linked from all four module sections plus `std.cancel` (which advertises "cooperative cancellation & timeouts" and would otherwise mislead), telling the reader to bound the operation itself. (2) **the cap stopped lying**: `chezzi test --timeout=300` reported **`PASS`** on a test that ran 5008 ms; a post-hoc check in `test_runner.rs` now upgrades an over-cap `Pass` to `TIMED-OUT` with a message that distinguishes it from a real abort (*"…ran 3002ms and could not be aborted…"*), and only `Pass` is upgraded — a `Fail`/`Error`/`OverMemory` keeps its more informative bucket. Verified: verdict, summary counts, `--fail-fast`, the quiet renderer, the JSON status and **exit 1** all treat it as a real timeout |
| ~~**W7-48**~~ | `:9525` | **FIXED 2026-08-12 — a `?`/`?.` inside a `spawn:` block is now a compile error, whatever the enclosing fn returns.** `SpawnTarget::Block` (`src/checker/sig.rs:2668`) carried the enclosing frame's `current_ret`/`in_fn_body`/`recover_depth` across the task boundary, so a carrier-returning enclosing fn made the `?` **check clean** and the `Err` then early-returned out of the *task*, where the nursery discards it by design (`W7-46`) — a **silent swallow**. Measured pre-fix `chezzi check`: `-> Result[int,str]` **`ok: no type errors`**, module top level **`ok`**, `-> Option[int]` rejected but naming the *enclosing* fn, nil fn rejected by luck. **A second hole, found while fixing:** `recover: parallel: spawn: v := g()?` was also `ok` — `recover_depth` crossed the boundary too, so the `?` was typed against a `recover:` boundary in a *different frame*; measured runtime (both engines, rc=0) `recover result: Ok(nil)`, `boom` nowhere — the recover reported **success** for an error it never saw | **Fix:** the task boundary zeroes `in_defer_block` + `recover_depth` and sets a new `in_spawn_block`, whose gate in `infer_try` sits **after** the `recover:`/`defer:` gates and **before** the return-kind match — so a `recover:`/`defer:` OUTSIDE the task falls through and is rejected, while one nested INSIDE re-arms from zero and stays legal (its boundary is in the same frame as the `?`). `current_ret`/`in_fn_body` are deliberately **NOT** zeroed: `in_spawn_block` gates `infer_try` before either is read, and their only other reader is `check_return` — an earlier cut that zeroed them made `spawn: return 5` inside `fn main() -> int` emit a second, FALSE `function returns nothing, cannot return a value`, the very enclosing-fn lie this row exists to remove (caught by adversarial review, two independent prosecutors, fixed + pinned by `spawn_block_return_does_not_claim_the_enclosing_fn_returns_nothing`). The gate is shaped like its siblings — a non-carrier operand keeps `'?' expects Result or Option, found int` and an `Unknown` operand does not cascade. Message: `'?' is not allowed inside a spawn block: a spawned task has no caller to propagate to`, matching the existing `'{kw}' is not allowed inside a spawn block` for `return`/`break`/`continue` (a `?` IS a return). **Reject-widening blast radius, measured before landing:** exactly 4 sites in all 386 `.chz` files (`tests/chz/spec/opt_carrier_test.chz` §9, added by `W7-43` to assert the buggy acceptance) — rewritten to keep their intent by moving the `?.` into the `parallel:` body (parent frame), asserted behaviour `Ok(20)` unchanged. Six `ok()` neighbours pinned: `?` in a `parallel:` body, in a nested `fn`, in a closure, in a `defer:`/`recover:` inside the task, and `spawn f(g()?)` (args evaluate in the parent) |
| ~~**W7-49**~~ | `:9600` | **FIXED 2026-08-11 — `Span` carries a `file: u32`, so the three checker→compiler side tables are keyed injectively ACROSS MODULES again.** The hole: a default-parameter expression spliced from one module into another keeps the DEFINING module's spans but was keyed with the CALLING module's index, so two unrelated call sites at the same `line:col` shared one entry and the later insert won — a silent wrong value under a green `chezzi check`, identical on both engines (parity is blind). `KeywordKey`, `WitnessKey` and `CarrierKey` (`src/checker/ty.rs:24,43,109`) all share the tuple `(graph_module_idx, frag_ctx, frag_ord, key_span)`, and `Span` was `{line, col}` with **no file identity** (`src/lexer/mod.rs:148-152`). **Measured on `KeywordTable`, which had shipped for milestones — this was PRE-EXISTING, not introduced by `W7-43`:** `lib.chz:6 fn f(x: int = g(a=7, b=9)) -> int:` (first named-arg VALUE at col 19) + `main.chz:6     vvvvvv := g(b=1, a=2)` (first named-arg VALUE at col 19), both modules defining `fn h(a, b) = a*100+b` and `g := h` → `chezzi check` **clean**, `chezzi run` printed `709` then **`102`** where `probe()` is **`201`** (lib's identity permutation applied to main's reversed call). It now prints `709` / **`201`**. **All three tables are measured, each with a one-column-shift control that makes the fault vanish** — which is what isolates it to the KEY rather than the lowering. `CarrierTable`: an Option-mode `?.` inside a default colliding with a Result-mode `?.` at the same `line:col` gave the **Option** lowering to a **Result** carrier → `runtime error: no match arm for variant 'Ok'`. `WitnessTable`: **measured 2026-08-11, and it is mode (2) of the three that were predicted, NOT the worst-first (1)** — the wrong CONCRETE TYPE is constructed: `check` clean, then `no field 's' on Counter(n=7)` when main's `Other`-pinned `empty()` collides with lib's default `empty[Counter]()`. Neither the wrong-`argc` mode nor the false `internal:` compile error occurs when both colliding callees take one witness that resolves to a concrete key | **Fix as built: `Span` grew a `file: u32`** — assigned once at the single production lex seam `resolver::Builder::parse` (`src/resolver/mod.rs:~660`), `0` = synthesized/standalone; an interpolation fragment inherits its enclosing literal's id; `LoadedModule.file` records it, and a resolver invariant test asserts every module's id is non-zero, pairwise distinct, and stamped on that module's own spans. **The three key tuples and all nine record/lookup sites are UNCHANGED** — the spliced clone already carried the definer's spans and therefore now carries the definer's file id, so both halves keep deriving the key from the same AST with no new state to agree about. Nesting composes with **no stack**. `Span` went 16 → 24 → **12** bytes (`u32` line/col/file): the 24-byte intermediate regressed the `map` bench AND pushed `parser::MAX_DEPTH` and `vm::VM_STACK_BYTES` off their calibrated margins, so the width is now pinned by `lexer::tests::span_stays_twelve_bytes` (`Proto.lines` holds one `Span` per OPCODE, so this is hot VM data). Bench deltas: flat-or-faster everywhere except `map`, and a padding control shows that delta is **alignment, not size** — see `docs/contributing/benchmarks.md`. **Two guards landed with it.** (1) The `KeywordTable` lookup now HARD-ERRORS on a miss instead of falling through to `Op::Call(args.len())`, which silently **dropped every named argument** where the carrier (`:3852`) and witness (`:4548`) lookups already faulted; one shared `Compiler::keyword_perm` serves the call, `spawn` and `defer` sites. `print(sep=…, end=…)` — the one path that deliberately leaves `named` populated with no table entry — returns from the `prelude_fn` arm long before any of them, and `spawn`/`defer` of a named `print` is already a type error. (2) The three record sites go through `checker::record_call_table_entry`, which refuses to overwrite a key bound to a DIFFERENT value and surfaces the conflict as a hard `CompileError` (the harvest pass discards type errors, so the conflict travels as a 4th return value of `resolve_call_tables`). It compares **VALUES, never presence**: a same-key/same-value re-insert is the NORMAL case (two call sites omitting one default), and a presence-checking guard would reject every program with a same-module default. **Rejected:** a `Spliced` marker `ExprKind` (~12 walker arms, three of them capture/free-variable walkers where a missed arm is silent, *plus* it still needs the cross-half discriminator it was meant to avoid); a recomputable arg-position discriminator (~25 checker + 5 compiler arg-walk sites, unenforced, and the compiler's permuted loops walk a different order — needs a stack that a `(ctx, ord)` pair cannot express); re-spanning the spliced clone (**rejected twice already** — `5642b55d`/`67e848a6`: a span here IS a table key, and a flat re-span aliases two carriers inside ONE default). **Residual — CLOSED 2026-08-13 by `W7-51`, which is the very fix this row named:** the same default spliced twice into the SAME module kept one set of spans and therefore one key; a default was cloned into the CALLER's scope, so a caller-side local could shadow the definer's global and the two splices genuinely resolved differently (measured on `19f7696a`: `709` / **`907`** where both should be `709`; on `b1307258` the backstop turned that into a hard error). Compiling a default ONCE in its defining module is what landed, and it also removes the **divergence from CPython** — which evaluates defaults at `def` time in the DEFINING scope — so the same source now prints `709` / `709`, exactly as Python does. The value-comparing backstop stays as defence in depth under a key that is now STRUCTURALLY injective: a non-literal default expression exists exactly once in the whole program. **Evidence note:** the corpus contains **zero** cross-module spliced non-literal defaults, so the 978-run pre/post sweep proved only *no regression* — it proves nothing about the fix working. The proof rests on the measured repro and on four new Rust tests (`src/vm/tests.rs` `a_spliced_default_*` + the residual backstop, each failing on `19f7696a` and passing now) plus `tests/chz/spec/default_splice_keys_test.chz` for the two guards' over-fire modes **Class cross-ref (2026-08-16): a FOURTH instance of the span-keyed side-table aliasing class landed and was fixed — see the `span-keyed side table, instance #4` row. The class now reads M24-6 / W7-49 / W7-43 / ListWidenTable, and every fix made the coordinate REAL rather than re-anchoring a span.** |
| ~~**W7-47**~~ | `:9328` | **FIXED 2026-08-12 — `os.exit` from an eager `Executor` job now halts the process while `main` is blocked, instead of sitting in a slot nobody reads.** Measured on the gap's own repro, release binary, `timeout 12`: **socket-blocked `main` rc=124 (hang) → rc=3**; the `Channel` variant **rc=1 (`recv on an empty channel: deadlock`) → rc=3** — so the socket was only the *reachable* example, not the trigger. Go is rc=3 on both shapes | **Fix:** the run already owns a shared, non-static cell — `Vm::quiesce: Arc<QuiesceState>` (`src/vm/mod.rs:926`), cloned into every worker at `sched.rs:3572`, and documented at `quiesce.rs:45-49` as per-run precisely because `cargo test` runs many VMs in one process. `request_exit` publishes the masked code there beside the per-`Vm` `pending_exit`, and a new `Vm::run_exit_err` rung lets the blocking loops adopt it — `pending_exit` set plus the `"exit"` sentinel, verbatim the shape `reduce_task_slots` already produces, so recover-bypass, defer-skipping and the driver are all unchanged code. **No `std::process::exit`** (`stream.rs:17-20` refuses it: library code, racing writer threads, and a thread that kills the process discards the run's outcome) and **no wakeup plumbing** — every counted-party wait is already a bounded ≤5 ms poll (`DEMOTE_POLL_BACKOFF`), so the flag IS the wakeup, and once each job's own wait unwinds the untimed `join_eager_jobs` condvar releases through the mechanism it already has. **The rung sits BELOW `cancel_requested` and ABOVE the deadlock verdict**: below cancel so a party already holding a cancel flag still unwinds as `Cancelled` (only a party with no cancel flag at all — precisely top-level `main` — reaches it), above the verdict because an `Exit` outranks a synthesized `Deadlocked`. `--serial` is bit-for-bit unchanged (it never blocks on a socket and never sets `eager_core`). **Two defects caught by adversarial review, both fixed before landing:** (1) the cell was never cleared, and `chezzi test` reuses ONE `Vm` per file — a `test fn` calling `os.exit` made every later blocking `test fn` `ERROR … exit` (cleared in `invoke_test`, pinned by `a_test_fn_that_exits_does_not_poison_later_blocking_tests`); (2) `reduce_task_slots` read `first_exit` only from task SLOTS, so an eager job — which owns no slot — was invisible and a nursery reported `deadlock` instead of the exit (`first_exit.or_else(|| self.quiesce.pending())`, pinned by `eager_job_os_exit_beats_a_blocked_nurserys_deadlock_verdict`; no existing exit test's expected value moved). **Residual ceilings filed as `W7-57`**; a genuinely PRE-EXISTING false deadlock found while investigating is filed as `W7-56` |
| ~~**W7-46**~~ | `:9247` | **FIXED 2026-08-10 — the SEMANTICS were measured correct and Go-consistent; the defect was two examples asserting an unearned success.** A spawned fn's returned `Err` is discarded while a raw fault aborts the nursery. Measured Go 1.26.5: `go func(){ _ = errors.New("boom") }()` → `main finished normally`, **rc=0** (error dropped — this is why `errgroup` exists); `go func(){ panic("boom") }()` → `panic: boom`, `exit status 2`. **Chezzi matches Go on BOTH channels**, so the "disagreement" is the Go contract, not a bug — and `Result` is a value, so there is nowhere for a statement-form `spawn`'s return to go. The real defect: `examples/echo_server.chz` / `echo_server_spawn.chz` printed a **hard-coded** `echo server handled 50 connections` at rc=0 having handled zero on `--serial`, and 3 tests asserted that vacuous line | **Fix:** both examples now count what actually happened — a shared `AtomicInt` (Go's `atomic.Int64`) bumped only by a client whose full round-trip returned `echo:ping`, read after the nursery joins. `--serial` now truthfully prints `echo server handled 0 connections`; the default engine prints the real `50`. `parity_tests.rs` `echo_server_example_terminates_on_the_serial_engine` asserts the honest `0`; `net_echo_server_services_more_conns_than_workers` + `net_echo_server_spawns_handler_per_connection` assert `all served: 100` instead of a vacuous `contains("all served")`. `docs/reference/concurrency.md` states the two-channel contract once, with the measured Go output |
| ~~**W7-45**~~ | `:9487` | **FIXED 2026-08-11 — the `where`-bound hole `W7-41` closed for `==` is closed on the builtins whose runtime is `values_equal` too, on BOTH halves.** With `W7-41`'s `struct Box[T]` carrying `fn eq(...) where T: Comparable` over a non-`Comparable` `Tag`, all of `[a].contains(b)`, `[a].index_of(b)`, `[a, b].dedup()`, `[a, b].unique()`, `a in [b]`, a `Set` element and a `Map` key used to be check-clean then `runtime error: struct 'Tag' has no 'compare' method` — and the Set/Map half faulted **only on a hash collision**, so with distinct hashes the same unsound program silently succeeded with a wrong answer (measured: `m := {a: 1}` printed `1`, rc=0). The checker types the operand, picks Option-vs-Try, then **clone-lowers to a real AST shape and infers THAT** — so the Result-mode clone literally *contains* an `ExprKind::Try` and all three of `infer_try`'s gates (`recover_depth`, `in_defer_block`, the `current_ret`/`in_fn_body` return-kind gate) apply with **zero new gate code**: the row's own predicted cost centre was discharged by construction. The choice is recorded in a `CarrierTable` keyed `(module_idx, frag_ctx, frag_ord, **name_span**)` — a NEW `OptChain` field, load-bearing because `parse_postfix` takes `let span = e.span;` once before the postfix match, so every link of `a?.b?.c` shares the primary's span and a **mixed** chain (`a: Result`, `a.b: Option`) would have aliased two different modes onto one entry under a green `chezzi check`. The compiler looks the mode up and clone-lowers with the SAME shared functions (`desugar::lower_carrier_option`/`lower_carrier_try`), so spans, keys and bytecode cannot drift; a MISSING entry is a hard `CompileError`, never a default. **The VM is unchanged** (the Result path emits the `Op::Try` the spaced form already emitted); a runtime-dispatched `CarrierEnter`/`CarrierExit` opcode pair was **rejected** — two opcodes and two new serial-vs-M:N surfaces for zero benefit, since the checker must decide statically anyway. `??` stays **Option-only** (no spaced alternative to rescue, no ancestor offers it, and it would silently discard an error payload) with ONE accurate error naming `??` and pointing at `match`. Migration papercut: `f()?.len() ?? 0` on a Result is now an error on the `??`. Evidence: 3991 lib tests green; `tests/chz/spec/opt_carrier_test.chz` (21 `test fn`) green on BOTH engines, every case an **equivalence** assertion between the two spellings (a widening is untestable by the suite that rejected it); plus a bytecode-**identity** test asserting `f()?.len()` and `f()? .len()` emit identical opcode sequences. Grammar productions unchanged. **Residuals filed as `W7-48`** (a `?` inside `spawn:`) **and `W7-49`** — `CarrierKey` inherits the cross-module key-aliasing hole that `KeywordKey`/`WitnessKey` have carried since they shipped; `name_span` makes the key injective *within* a module, which is all it ever claimed, and `W7-49`'s `Span`-file fix closes the rest without touching this tuple **Class cross-ref (2026-08-16): a FOURTH instance of the span-keyed side-table aliasing class landed and was fixed — see the `span-keyed side table, instance #4` row. | ~~**W7-41**~~ | `:8874` | **FIXED 2026-08-11 — `==` and `!=` enforce the receiver's `eq` `where` bounds, so the operator and the method spelling give one answer.** `struct Box[T]` with `fn eq(self, other: Self) -> bool where T: Comparable` over a non-`Comparable` `Tag` used to be `chezzi check` → *ok: no type errors*, `chezzi run` → `runtime error (line 7, col 12): struct 'Tag' has no 'compare' method` — the `checker-superset-of-compiler` class, which two-engine parity is structurally blind to. Verbatim on the post-change binary: *type error (line 15, col 7): cannot compare Box[Tag] and Box[Tag] for equality — Box[Tag]'s `eq` requires Tag: Comparable*. `Box[int]` still compares fine, which is the shape rustc 1.97.0 produces on the mirror (`E0369` on `Boxy<Tag>`, `Boxy(1) == Boxy(2)` compiling) | **THE ORDERING IS THE LESSON, and it is why the 2026-08-10 attempt was reverted.** That attempt was the same guard — *"the type declares an `eq` ⇒ require `satisfies(ty, Eq)`"* — added while `Eq` was still granted intrinsically to only the four scalars, so `where T: Eq` was unsatisfiable for every type whose `==` is structural: it shipped **fully green** (3952 lib tests, 424 Chezzi tests both engines, clippy clean, repro correctly rejected) and turned `Box([1,2])`, `Box((1,2))`, `Box(b"ab")`, `Box(Some(1))`, `Box(P(1))` from `true` into `cannot compare … for equality`. **Widening `Eq` is not a prerequisite bolted on beside the fix — it IS the fix's other half, and it had to land FIRST**, or the intermediate commit re-ships the reverted bug verbatim.
